Before I remind you of the procedure that we are going to follow today, I have the honour to announce that our meeting is being attended by the upper Western theories, after having been toured in the exhibition of the Chamber "ELLERIOS VENIZELOS" and informed about the history of the building and how to organize and operate the Parliament, twenty-four students and students and two educational sessions from the 1st Koropiou High School. The House welcomes them. [[Applause from all sides of Parliament]] Ladies and gentlemen, on a point of order, I would remind you that the debate on the Schedule Declarations will take place in accordance with Rule 141 of the Rules of Procedure, together with analogous application of Rules 96(2) and 97 which govern the debate on the bills, in accordance with the more specific decisions we have taken unanimously yesterday. So, as we decided unanimously yesterday, there will be a first-class seven-priority speakers, which will include one MEP from each parliamentary group. This is so that all parties can have a first placement before entering the circles of speakers. The MPs notified by the parties about this first circle of speakers are: From SYRIZA are Mr. Dimitris Vitsakis, from New Democracy Mr. George Koumoutsakos, from the People's Association - Golden Dawn Mr.Ilias Kashidiaris, from Potamis Antigoni Lyberakis, from the Communist Party of Greece Mr. Nikolaos Karathanasopoulos, from the Independent Greeks Mr. Dimitris Kammenos and from PASOK Mr.Gregorakos. I would also like to inform you that the parties have submitted lists of speakers, as provided for in Rule 65(5) of the Rules of Procedure. These speakers, as set out in the Rules of Procedure and as decided unanimously yesterday, will precede the speakers who will be registered with the electronic system. Those who wish to take part in the debate and their name are not included in the lists of their parties or if their party has not given a list, they will be registered with the electronic system, namely their card, from the start of the speech of the first speaker and until the end of the speech of the seventh speaker, as we unanimously decided yesterday. A single list of Members will then be drawn up in order to respect the proportion of parliamentary power in representing parties with circles of speakers. Each cycle will consist of thirteen MPs, involving five MPs from SYRIZA, three MPs from New Democracy, one MP from the People's Association - Golden Dawn, one MP from the Potami, one MP from the Communist Party of Greece, one MEP from the Independent Greeks and one MEP from PASOK. This is the ratio, according to the fractions and the measure provided for in the Rules of Procedure of the Parliament. The MPs of the League will alternate with the MPs of the Opposition. The turn to follow is: A SYRIZA MEP, a MP from New Democracy, a MP from SYRIZA, a MP from the Golden Dawn, a MP from SYRIZA, a MP from the New Democracy, a MP from SYRIZA, a MP from the Potami, a MP from SYRIZA, a MP from the Communist Party of Greece, a MP from the Independent Greeks, a MP from PASOK and a MP from the New Democracy. The Ministers, as we decided yesterday, will be speaking under the Rules of Procedure for up to eighteen minutes, the Deputy Ministers for Foreign Affairs up to fourteen minutes and the Undersecretaries up to nine minutes. If there is a Minister's place after which a Member of the Council of Ministers is followed, there will be a rotation so that there is no violation of the Rules of Procedure. I would remind you that we unanimously decided yesterday that parliamentary representatives could speak for up to twelve minutes in their prototypes, regardless of whether the leader of their party has been given a speech, six minutes in the second and three minutes in the third. I would also remind you that registered speakers have the right to take the floor for seven minutes, without the right to secondary education, as we decided yesterday. I would ask that, in order to avoid any involvement with the listing system on the list of speakers, do not place your card as those you wish to register, only when I tell you that the registration is starting now. For young people and new colleagues, I inform you that as long as there is an indication ‘Start of Registration’ you can also be entered when there is an indication ‘Start of Registration’ the list has been completed. With these introductory words we will now enter the first seven priority speakers. I will give the floor to Mr Dimitris Vitsa.

ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u6	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I am going directly to the point, stressing very much five points. Point 1 : The last five years in Greece in particular - as an 'experimental animal' - but also throughout Europe, are opposed to two sensible, two policies with very practical results. The first one, which is the applicable one, recovers and implements the SOS scenario of the super-profits of the few, the rescue scenario of the former system that sees the way out through extreme austerity, the dissolution of the social state, the commercialisation of social goods and public property, internal devaluation. The second looks at the way out into the real economy, the commitment to the service of basic social needs and the protection of the environment, the strengthening of social solidarity. We deny that in order to settle public finances and create a new social and economic cohesion, a'strategic' of one and a half million unemployed had to be created, bundled into about three hundred thousand businesses, living without shelter, without access to energy, and fed to social bundles of the municipalities and the Church. We think there is an alternative policy, another way. And he is indicated by our programme, as it is drawn up by the Programming Declarations. On the 25th of January this second political proposal had a historic victory. We intend, with the implementation of our programme, to make it a historic day. Point 2: I will not trouble you with the repetitions. Besides, the Prime Minister spoke in detail yesterday. But I want you to focus on the internal cohesion of our programme. The humanitarian crisis response programme favours the economic relaunch, which in turn strengthens work, in a context of the country's democratic and cultural regeneration. And all this in a single national and social project with a stable European orientation. With this plan, we say clearly who we are. We are with the poor, the unemployed, the new generation, the farmers, the small and medium-sized ones and the small ones. With this plan we reclaim the right of society to plan collectively, to record, process, implement, control and criticise. We are therefore applying a social salvation plan that is firmly in place in the current reality and is evolving ahead of policies that extend to the sector, industry, space and time. We refuse to hand over the design to the interests of over-profit. We refuse to subject social cohesion, economic stability and development to the designs of the 'triangle of sin', which is even trying to take advantage of the global economic crisis meeting with the economic crisis that it has created for many decades. Point 3: Our homeland is a rich place that the "three-round of sin" plunders. And because home is first and foremost people, people are looting at the same time as people's lives. This will change with no matter how much it takes! The party and client state are over. The public who works for private interests is over! At the party of corruption and interlocutory lights are gone! No one, of course, should be afraid of doing his job well. But he must lose his sleep who thinks things change to stay the same. Old habits are cut off, and as we previously called upon society not to become accustomed to poverty, we invite it not to get used to each dirt, to rival it and clean it up. We will not rest if the new role of the citizen is not sought in some dreams, but it will be everyday practice at work, at the place of residence, at the place of education. The expression 'I am asking for the floor' is the basis of democracy. And democracy can be - and it will be - effective both socially and economically. We are looking forward to a Europe of convergence, solidarity, democracy and peace. We're working on it. We are opposed to an alternative draft of radical changes and we are not avoiding, nor leaving, from the field where ideas, values and policies are confronted. We respect the rules of the framework that we are trying to change. It would be a great mistake, however, to assume that this respect is a weakness. For us, the keywords are'solid order', 'decisive', 'new agreement', 'justice' and 'prosperity'. In our vocabulary there is not the word'submission', but - let it be understood - there is not even the word 'bluff'. And we have allies strong: the social need and the new reality, the social and political forces who are increasingly seeing the deadlock and are shouting against austerity. This cry will be heard on Wednesday by a united people and by everyone whose hearts are pulverising the pace of change, throughout Europe, throughout the world. And this outcry, this policy will stand a barrier to projects that jeopardise European cohesion, which undermine extreme right-wing logic and practices and the fascist monster. Point 5: The Government of Social Rescue, but also SYRIZA, did not come to just fight. It came to remain, in order for dignity, democracy, development on the basis of social needs, to remain social cohesion and unity. Hope is here and the future declares its presence in voting for the programme commitments - not declarations - and the government. Thank you.

We have to warn all of this about the fact that it is not enough in Europe to say that it has the people's mandate, because all the governments themselves can invoke it. So don't jeopardize our position in the Eurozone. Don't patronize our homeland. Don't start dismantling everything with huge sacrifices we've achieved. Also, don't dare prescribe '81 transcripts and attempts to reunite. ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u16	Thank you, Madam President. The JRC will not question the Prime Minister's commitment yesterday to negotiating hard with the partners. In fact, the negotiation made by the government, as part of the management of Greece's large state debt, forms part of the more general negotiation between the European Union's urban ranks for the management of the overcompensated states and the overall future of the Eurozone. The ruling class of Italy, France and other highly indebted states wants to liberate more state resources in order to support domestic monopolistic groups and their investments in order to shield their competitiveness. In other words, they call for a reduction in the size of state funding which is currently committed to serving the State debt and for strict conditions for zeroing deficits in the annual budgets to be relaxed. With the support of the United States of America, the Italian and French Governments are putting pressure on the German Government to bear a greater burden on them to accept the joint management of part of the debt of the euro area's highly indebted States. They are, in other words, trying to reduce the gap in growth that is constantly expanding for the benefit of Germany in the context of international competition. The United States' support for pressure on the German Government is about the general strife for the most imperialist centre to take over the next period in Europe. It also concerns the competition of the dollar and the euro which are international reserves. ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u22	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, democracy is not part of an economy, society, culture, security. Ladies and gentlemen, we are living in a new context of crime and security. We have very high levels of violence that directly affect human life. Most of all, however, we have a crime which offends the security of citizens and poses a risk to democracy itself. That is why the rule of law - and above all the police and justice - must find out how they will manage this new environment of crime and insecurity, in terms of constitutional and democratic legitimacy. The new form of violence, crime and insecurity creates new roles in the police strategy, which must also take into account social and cultural data, collective consciousness and, of course, the history and rights of the people, and that is why? Because the effective exercise of social control by the police presupposes a minimum of political democracy and legitimacy, but also a minimum of social consensus. The police in a democratic state protects citizens' rights while in an authoritarian state it oppresses citizens. The triangular relationship between the state - police - citizen is therefore, in our view, confronted by the fact that the obligation of police to protect the democratic constitution in the context of constitutional order is a timeless democratic demand. Critical to the question: Can the police, in addition to social control, also be charged with establishing democratic legitimacy, perhaps with ensuring social cohesion? The police as a social function, but also as an institutional function, can aim to protect and regulate the social order, namely the area of the exercise of the rights of all of us? Today things seem to change. The citizens do not only want protection from crime, but also from generalised inflexibility and lawlessness. The police should therefore not only deal with the incidents, but should prevent social events, social tensions before they become criminals. Therefore, identifying the problem and, above all, the causes created by the new police action means scientific policing and community policing. I'll come back. This multidisciplinary cooperation between local institutions, social services, citizens and the police - a square that is not always an easy case - is worth seeing and trying. Ladies and gentlemen, in addition to proper administration, which presupposes the appropriateness of action and effectiveness, the parameters remain critical: legality of intervention, the proportionality of measures and the social acceptance of the result. The exact definition of what legal democratic police intervention means, namely a positive response from police and citizens, must be studied in depth. The police, however, need to integrate policing into the community, by preventing and preventing it, by mediation and, above all, by regulating and not suppressing social problems. On the political side it might be desirable to set up a national policing council, which will draw up a general strategy and refer to Parliament. I'll come back to this. On the scientific side, the time has come - I believe - to establish a research institute for the study and analysis of crime and police studies. We must prevent the dynamics of the crime and not run behind it. Nothing, of course, can do without changing the social profile of the Greek police officer. As we cannot have training without teachers, in the same way we cannot have police without police with a high sense of self-esteem, social recognition and guaranteed status. The modern policeman does not play hideout with thieves in the corners of the alana or the court, but he has to face the empire of crime, the so-called Killer capitalismus. In order to have a friend and a protector in the street, in the school and in the business, we must, on the one hand, trust him with a strategic role and not just an executive and, on the other hand, train him properly. This may be the beginning of a new era in the relationship between police and citizens, but also a new era in the right, modern, effective and always within the limits of our Constitution and our civilisation to deal with crime. For us there are no superiors and inferior people, service agents and institutions. There are high-level and small-level executives, but who are all low-paid, everyone deserves recognition of the risk of their profession, and yet everyone must be subject to disciplinary and democratic control. The Greek Police must understand that collective action is not a factor of disruption to public order, but of social function in so far as the police facilitates the exercise of the democratic rights of citizens. Relations between it and the citizens will be improved. Of course, this does not mean that the police will not deal drastically with cases of crime if this is necessary. However, this should be done on a different basis, where the police will not deal with the crowd either as homogeneous or in advance too dangerous. Every protester is not an enemy of the police, and this should be made absolutely clear to the police personnel managing mass demonstrations. Only by adopting such an approach can the relationships of trust between Greek police and Greek society be restored. The Prime Minister yesterday underlined "democracy everywhere", the new doctrine of security, freedom, rights and relations for police-citizens. Symbolic is the change in the title of the NSA to National Rulership Protection Service. However, it is important that he assumes responsibility for foreign security rather than internal police services. Development of the Centre for Security Studies at the Institute for Crime Policy and the establishment of a National Police Council for Police or possibly a Special Permanent Committee of the Parliament. In this context, it will be reassessed and improved the role of police special and on-board forces. We will see them again in the first place: because they were so engraved, because they had done so and how exactly society was finally faced with the police. Establishment of a Judiciary which will be subject to the Ministry of Justice. The dear Minister of Justice knows very well that there is Law 2145/93, twenty-two years ago, who is founding, but there is no presidential decree to operate. Reset the neighborhood officer's institution, pedestrian patrols. Why all this? They need us because the police are basically the protector of the poor. The Occupants and Occupants don't need public police. So, apart from all the other burdens we've loaded on him, he doesn't feel insecurity in the neighborhood, in the square, in the school. Of course, there will be upgrading and modernisation in the training of police officers. We need to review the institutional framework for the organisation of the police. Although, thirty years later, we voted in 2014 for a new law, it has malfunctioning problems. We have to fight corruption, with a change in disciplinary law. We need to make use of the former municipal officers as long as they want to stay. Upgrade the technological support for police and infrastructure work in general. To rethink the crisis, transfer and recruitment procedures with a view to merit. To reassess safety and hygiene issues. We've already released policemen from paradigms. Already five hundred policemen and eighty vehicles were returned, having evaluated only the politicians. We have three and a half thousand more cases of the evaluation of non-political persons. We are sure that in the end we will have over a thousand police officers on the street and not outside of homes. In the firefighting, there's a five-year deadline for firefighting firefighters. There is indeed a new heading. I'll come back to this. We've all cost it. We need to renew the fleet of national air fire-fighting instruments. We need to upgrade the Fire Academy, simplify the procedures for the supply of material, and always have health and safety conditions for businesses. They're all working. At the level of civil protection: strengthening humanitarian operational and operational assistance and addressing emergency needs, promoting voluntary civil protection and self-protection, awareness-raising on issues of population preparation and response and special groups, small and medium-sized enterprises, etc. I would like to stress, ladies and gentlemen, that we have had an extreme five days and have not heard - and it is a success of the firefighting - 'Where is the state?' I have also told the police that there will be no political exploitation of its successes. Police hits are police hits. For the police failures, I'll take responsibility. We will study the establishment of the institution of the Police Counselor, according to the standards of the other European countries, and there will be transparent, objective and effective control of alleged cases of racism, inhumane treatment or torture. The first bill will repeal the provisions of Law 4281 and Law 4254 on insurance matters for employees in the Security Body (TEPAS) and will henceforth apply the relevant statutes. We will repeal the Ministerial Decision based on Opinion No 4414 of the Legal Council of State regarding the extension beyond the eighteenth month of the detention of immigrants. I would like to point out that most of the proposals do not have financial costs. The first is institutional intervention. Where, however, a new burden arises, other than already provided for in the budget, it has been fully costed and covered by European funds and the ESDP. I will give you two - three examples. I'm going to talk to you about issues relating to vehicles - seized vehicles - because since 2000 it has to buy a vehicle for the police and the fire department. In 2014, we received two hundred three vehicles and we expect to get four hundred this year. From the ESRB, European funding, we received four hundred ninety in 2014 and this year we are waiting to get a hundred fifty and twenty-six of the Operational Programme. What I will mention below in the framework of the programme of the Ministry of Justice will in principle be based on some of the principles I propose. The Constitution will stand essentially at the top of the hierarchy of the rules of law. The practice of forwarding a memorandum, following a law and the Constitution to remain in the third place or to be sidelined, does not have a place in the politics and work of justice. Moreover, defending the Greek Constitution is at the same time defending the common constitutional traditions of Europe, which constitute primary Union law. Justice in its formation will be worthwhile and democratic, as will be analysed below. In the face of crime, there is no place for a zero tolerance policy, no matter how well the term is. Justice will be strict against guilty persons for serious crimes and lenient tolerant of petty offenders. Criminal justice is not punishing the mind, but guilty acts that have been committed. There will be equal treatment against the mind. Emphasis will be placed on crime prevention and reintegration, while prisons should be human, not only because of the need to respect human rights, but also because crime is reproduced. A leading institutional issue in the field of justice is certainly the beginning of the procedures for the revision of the Constitution. I would point out that officials of justice are being dealt with and, in any case, are, among other things, lawyers, notaries, judges and rangers, whose opinion will also be heard very carefully on their own issues, but also on the more general issues of justice. In general, there will be a search for reforms to be synchronised with changes in infrastructure, which should be able to support them. Our involvement in the European framework of institutions and especially in the Council of Ministers of Justice of the European Union in the formulation of common policies will primarily guarantee human rights. Also, the adaptation of Greek legislation to international contractual texts or directives will proceed on the basis of the country's contractual obligations, but also on the basis of a weighting of the social needs and, above all, of course, of our Constitution. A review of the terms of operation of the National School of Judges will take place, in particular by promoting institutions for the training of judges, with emphasis on racism, xenophobia and drugs. Measures to speed up the administration of justice will be promoted, which will not undermine the guarantees of rationality. This will be done in the context of the code reform, so that it does not cause systemic problems and is effective. Of course, there will always be an attempt to cover the empty organic positions of judges and judges. Combating corruption and crimes in general: It will be served by the functioning of justice to the extent of its competence and in cooperation with other relevant ministries and authorities. As the Prime Minister said yesterday, cases of economic corruption - Lagard, Liechtenstein and any available data - they will be investigated and promoted, as will claims of damages and mortgages by executing judicial decisions. There is an important advance at the moment to produce new codes. There will be an attempt to revise the existing plans on the basis of the principles that I have mentioned. At the moment, however, I can mention the following: The Sanctions Code Plan is currently more suited to the new circumstances and will be promoted as a priority, of course, after a short processing process. The draft Code of Civil Procedure includes, inter alia, arrangements for proof, deadlines and forced execution, seizures and auctions, which provide for speeds of execution of social and procedural hardships. These will be reviewed in the context of a comprehensive review, after strong responses from scientific associations and associations have been recorded for the content of this plan. The draft Code of Criminal Procedure includes useful reforms, but the current criminal procedure as a whole also includes arrangements such as those concerning the one-member criminal courts, which need to be reviewed in the context of a comprehensive review. The promotion of the plan of the Criminal Code - also important work - may be the result of careful consideration of the more general practices and sometimes chainy effects of certain reforms of the criminal system, such as those for the abolition of financial penalties, for example. There will be a reduction in the costs of access to justice, expenditure which prohibits the recourse of the weak and sometimes the average citizen to it. I am going through the chapter of the anti-crime policy and the penitentiary system. Even symbolically, let me first say that minor prisons will be effectively abolished. More specifically, the imposition of criminal incarceration on minors of 15 to eighteen years of age will be drastically restricted to provide for homicide crimes only. This attempt to revise will be made in the context of the revision of the Criminal Code, so that there will be no discrimination. The abolition of type III penitentiaries will proceed. The recently introduced institution is problematic in many respects. Firstly, it changes the sentence qualitatively, without this change always relying on a judgment, which means that we are in disparity with what was judged by the court as a fair recompense, as a fair penalty. Moreover, the conditions for entering the C-type penitentiary were so wide that the majority of detainees and deprivations, for example the right to communication, were threatened with an appeal to fundamental human rights. The abolition, of course, does not mean that there cannot be wards with increased security measures in various penitentiaries. I would also remind you that the best Greek teachers abroad are presently taking part in the Foundation Councils today, the most important of them, who are transferring their experience and which often come most of the time without even getting their tickets, and of course without pay. It is very important and the role they play during the elections of the rectoral and secular authorities, because their intervention, the pre-selection of three candidates who are then voted on by the members of the universities, the teachers, is to ensure that the transaction, which had become our universities in the situation we know, is avoided. It would be a catastrophic mistake to return the election of the rectal and secular authorities to student and administrative associations, as was the case before. I would remind you that students and administrative staff are well represented in the Senate and have the opportunity to take part in the debate and in decisions on their issues. Parliament must understand that universities have long been a man of law. How would you look today if the doors suddenly opened and entered here a crowd of young people who would ask you out of the Chamber to use it for a convention of their own? How would you like to go to the President's office tomorrow morning by a group of students or administrative officials and ask her to stop her work and adopt the decisions they want to impose? How would you like to follow preconvulsions, insults and even gestures? This is the day-to-day reality in universities, and whoever does not understand it, does not understand how our universities operate today and it is absolutely necessary to take care to get the universities back to legality. What happened three days ago at the University of Athens, where again a bunch of students and administrators entered the Senate, dismantled it, expelled the senators and then forced the rector to make decisions in their own name, showing the fate in which we have come. It is absolutely necessary to realise that this kind of behaviour must be stopped, because the dismantling of public universities precedes the dismantling of the country. I would also like to say that I wondered because in the Prime Minister's Programming Statements we did not hear any positive measures on the reconstruction and relaunching of universities. We have not heard any new ideas which will allow us to raise the universities where we have to get them. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] However, we could have many such ideas. The idea of a university pole for the reconstruction of university division, the concept of framework contracts, which we could adopt to encourage the universities to achieve objectives, the concept of abandoning the collective distribution of cognitive objects, so that the degrees can give not just the departments, but the schools, were matters that I expected to hear and I hope that the Minister of Education will include them in the dialogue that will begin. I shall conclude, Madam President, in a minute. I shall no longer refer to what I have been thinking about middle schooling and the schools of the first grade of education and the kindergartens. I would just like to stress that we are often listening to the concept of equal education. I would like to stress that we need to return our education - and the supreme and the middle - to the concept of excellence. This means that, firstly, every idea of equal education should be abandoned. We will have to return to the experimental schools of excellence, not to enter these schools in a compensatory way, and the war in private education will have to cease, because it is the right of every citizen to choose the type of education he wishes to receive. I fear that what we have heard about education from the Programming Declarations, but also what we have understood from the election commitments, give the slogan for our education'superiorly backwards' and that is a great risk to the state. Thank you very much.

And it is important that there is already a transition to the rhetoric of the government, at least SYRIZA - I am not sure of the views of the Independent Greeks on this point - from nominal debt deletion to an indirect haircut, with an extension of the repayment period and a reduction in interest rates, so that we can end up with an indirect debt write-off, which can facilitate cash flows over the next few years. This is a pillar in which we can invest. You can, however, press on this perspective, precisely because the previous government, this party, the New Democracy, succeeded with great sacrifices by the citizens, to achieve primary surpluses, which you have invoked today. Because if there were no primary surpluses, there would be no agreement in November 2012, which allows us to discuss this indirect debt haircut today. So step on an achievement of the previous government. You do well and press on this achievement, but it is good sometimes when you talk about a rugged economy to recognise and that the rectification of public finances, which was achieved with great sacrifices by Greek citizens, was a success throughout the country and therefore a success on which you can invest. The second pillar of a possible agreement is the smaller primary surpluses. Yes, I agree with you, and I think many economists also agree that it is practically impossible for an economy that is slowly coming back on course of recovery to produce primary surpluses of 4.5%. And you will have to negotiate smaller primary surpluses of 1.5% -2%, which will be able to free you from budgetary resources to policy. And of course, there are ideological differences. We would prefer these surpluses to return them to the citizens, through a reduction in taxes, while you may choose something different. In any case, however, the objective of achieving smaller budgetary surpluses is possible. The generally acknowledged rules of international law, as well as international conventions, since their ratification by law and entry into force under the terms of each of them, form an integral part of the domestic Greek law and prevail from any other law. In order to serve a great national interest and to promote cooperation with other States, it may be recognised, by treaty or agreement, to the institutions of international organisations provided for in the Constitution. A three-fifths majority of the total number of Members is required to vote in favour of this treaty or agreement. 3. Greece shall freely, by means of a law passed by an absolute majority of the total number of Members, restrict the exercise of its national sovereignty, as long as it is dictated by a great national interest, do not affect human rights and the foundations of the democratic culture and shall be made on the basis of the principles of equality and reciprocity." I would simply remind you that the overall Memorandum has passed without such provisions, i.e. without ratification of signature agreements, with legislative acts and, of course, without the procedure laid down in Article 28 of the Constitution. The second is the termination of the unpaid work of seafarers, a phenomenon which mainly concerns the coastline and even appears on vessels operating on a public-support basis. At the meeting with the representatives of the Vessel Union, we have taken the assurance that at the latest by Easter there will be no seafarer unpaid. We are already studying combined measures to miss this phenomenon. But we already have a problem and the Piraeus - Kythera - Antikytheros connection line is threatened by such a cause. They must all take their responsibilities. We'll take them. The third is the smooth and regular conduct of all scheduled routes and the minimisation of cases of non-implementation by any reason. We have already decided to carry out technical checks so that very soon the Ministry will have a full picture of the timely prevention of defects that are escaping ships. This is a practice that had been successfully implemented in the past and had been interrupted in recent years. At the same time, we aim at this first phase of planning in the reopening of high-definition lines, which have been interrupted for several reasons, such as trying to reconnect Ithaca and Kefalonia with Patras. Our policy, of course, does not end here. In fact, we must - and that is what we will do - include what has been said and much more in a medium-term planning in all areas of the Ministry's responsibility. For this medium - long-term planning I will refer to critical areas: firstly, shipping podium is undoubtedly the most dynamic sector of Greek shipping activity. The fact that this is an internationalised activity does not at all reduce the advantages that the Greek flag ensures. We want the Greek flag strong in global shipping. However, it is clearly a one-sided decline in the contribution of the Greek state and of the Greek society, especially seafarers, to this dynamism. Especially today, in the conditions of the economic and social destruction that our people are experiencing, there is an urgent need to increase the return of armaments to Greek society. This return must include, above all, strengthening employment, as mentioned above, but also expanding on other issues. We have already announced our commitment to stop privatising the PPA and the Thessaloniki Port Authority and guaranteeing the public character of the ports of the country. The debate on the development of the public port industry in the direction of the country's productive reconstruction has already begun so that through a generalised consultation ports can become strategic axes in the new period. Where we have already established the exploitation of ports or of marina by private individuals, the four prerequisites are: firstly, the safeguarding of labour rights; secondly, the protection of the environment; thirdly, the safeguarding of small and medium-sized enterprises in this production activity, with a view to increasing added value and social benefit; and fourthly, transparency. The integration of major ports into the Trans-European Transport Network, the exploitation of workers and their experience in this process, the effective connection of ports with local communities and their involvement in management and control of their operation are some of the prerequisites for the overall project. The deliberate depreciation of public ports over a number of years, with the aim of privatising them, has caused explosive and urgent problems. There are ports, particularly Heraklion, Volos and Thessaloniki, which, due to the lack of staff of critical specialists, are threatened with collapse. All port agencies will be called upon to put this issue first, much more so that its solution will improve both productive activity and profitability. In the same direction, we will also be moving towards supporting and reorganising the Navigation Agency, which also brings revenues, of course, as a critical public service. It is also a critical issue for the large ports, and above all Piraeus, that the installation of a ship power system is also a crucial issue, so that the operating costs for the vessels on the coast are reduced and environmental protection is strengthened. Finally, an urgent matter for the operation of ports is the management of ship waste, by eliminating the phenomena of opaque monopolistic practices, which also bears the costs of operating ships, and environmental protection becomes non-existent, but at the same time unacceptable phenomena of interoperable and corruption are being created. Already, after the attempt at corruption at the Port Authority of Corfu, we are going on to review all the waste management contracts in all the ports of the country. Of course, the existing institutional framework will also be reviewed. Our design also includes the public navigation network and the reform of the institutional framework. An important tool will be the public maritime transport operator, an objective that we set for consultation. The island links with waterplanes will also add new possibilities for the development of the islands and not only. Secondly, seafaring with guaranteed rights, reduced compositions, operational contracts, the safety ship and many other neoliberal cuts or even memorandum inventions lead to depreciation of work and have no development benefits, as has already been demonstrated. Thirdly, public structures to find maritime work will be supported to effectively hit the brokerage exploitation circuits. Fourthly, the recruitment of seafarers is a definitive past. Fifthly, modernising the maritime trade union movement is an overtime demand. Sixth, the objective is to guarantee, redesign and enhance public free maritime education on the basis of modern requirements, to exploit trained training staff and to support substantially the particular contribution of seafarers, to extend maritime education and to inferior crews, to support student living conditions. Seventh, guarantee pensions and health care for seafarers and their families. NAT must be able to collect the insurance contributions due. It needs modernization and staffing. It has other problems, both institutional and regulatory. We'll see them in the next space. Eighth, the Natus House can and must have a separate place in the primary care system, ensuring the priority service of seafarers. The public body of the ship repair industry is a difficult task, but it is extremely necessary. The large shipyards want a great reorganization. The Shipbuilding Zone in Perama and the smaller shipyards cannot survive and develop unless they are integrated into a centralised design. All of this refers to our overall view as the Ministry of Economy - formerly developed - and that is why one ministry complements the other under one general supervision, which we can organise general strategies. The aim is to strengthen short sea shipping, support professional tourist boats, generalised upgrading of island policy by the activation of the Institute and Council of Island Policy, the application of the Isle of Insularity clause to important legislation, the generalisation of the responsibility of the Ministry throughout the island and not just in the Aegean, with a series of targeted combined interventions. Sustainable maritime connections and island intercom are a constant priority. In particular, for small islands, a crucial issue is also their integrated public water supply and the provision of health and education services. For the new Greece to be born, islanders must stay on their islands. Ladies and gentlemen, at this point I shall make a special reference to the Hellenic Coast Guard. We are aiming at upgrading its social mission, ensuring its democratic functioning and the realisation of its members. Our aim is to redesign the organisation and operation of the Hellenic Coast Guard - Greek Coast Guard to deal effectively with the current challenges, regarding organised crime, smuggling, tax evasion, maritime safety, protection of the sea environment, control of sea borders, research and rescue at sea and others. All this requires serious effort and serious involvement with time-consuming issues, overripe, which remain unsolved. Legal system for classification, education, retraining, hierarchy, developments and crises. This issue is very important for a democratic body that has to carry out such a mission. Introduction of executives to productive schools in a democratic, fair and non-discriminatory manner. Practices that have given rise to many complaints about the diabetic of examinations in the past, I hope that they will actually be a past and we will try in every way and commit ourselves in this direction. The upgrading of education and retraining, the writing of modern writings on productive schools - which I would like to inform you that they do not even exist - are high-compensation investments in line with modern needs. There are still a number of other issues. In our view, it is also necessary to support the representatives of the Port Authority in a variety of ways, and one of these is the maintenance of the Relief Fund, which is not charged with the state budget, and of course directly the granting of the necessary extension by 30-6-2015, in order to achieve the ultimate objective of abolishing Article 220 of the Treaty. 4281/2014 and to restore the functioning of a assistance and support that there is no reason to be abolished. Care to ensure that there is no surprise, no overthrow of family planning and much more. In front of us is a step forward, we will face it. Ladies and gentlemen, in order for all these plans - and many others - to be implemented, of course - and the necessary reform measures, we need a different Ministry of Shipping and the Aegean, without irrationalism, without imperialism, without confusing roles and responsibilities. The planning services of all maritime sectors must be gradually and, after a serious study and consultation of the Ministry's political structures, with the upgraded use of both the civil staff and the representatives of the Port Authority with invaluable expertise and experience. In this direction, our programming objectives are: Upgrading the role of the Ministry's civil staff. The creation of new functional organograms. The development of necessary tools for the development of the NSRP by strengthening the staffing and certification of the relevant management. Institutional and operational interventions to control ships, to ensure the safety of human life at sea and to prevent accidents, to protect the marine environment. Special care for the staffing and upgrading of the Maritime Inspection and the exploitation of e-government capabilities in critical commercial maritime operating areas. It is not possible to proceed directly to such shallow interventions. However, these are designs that will be in place right now, so that they can be studied at the right time. All of this relates to the management and register of registered vessels, the management of shipping operations, the issue of ship certificates required for departure and harmonisation with international regulations. Ladies and gentlemen, I have completed the presentation of the main programming positions for the Ministry of Shipping and the Aegean. From now on, on the basis of this framework, and in difficult circumstances and in cooperation with all those involved in shipping and the Ministry, we are starting to try to solve problems and remove chronic diseases. Our commitment is to work for a democratic ministry and for a policy of navigation and insularity. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u104	Ladies and gentlemen, two weeks ago the citizens voted for a very important step towards the democratic emancipation of Greek society. Political change in the country gave a vital breath of national dignity to the people of a country affected economically, socially and politically. These days, the government of Greece, with its weapon, unprecedented support for society inside, the vast front against austerity in Europe and the enormous international interest in developments in Greece, will give to the end the battle to defend the interests of Greece. Our negotiating power lies in the huge stock of political credibility that has a government without commitments and dependencies on diversity of interests and with a fresh popular mandate to cancel the Memorandum, change priorities, social relief and reconstruction of the state economy and institutions, but at the same time also to restore the sense of law and political ethos in the country. By exploiting this valuable political chapter of collective self-confidence and hope, which has been rekindled in society, with determination, seriousness, studied steps, with strategic intransigence and tactical flexibility, we are sure to achieve it. The Memorandum has already seriously and unequally harmed the health of the citizens. The drastic reduction in public spending has caused dissolutionary effects in the health system, making the daily lives of workers and patients grim. In the past few years, we have not only had a management of less resources due to budgetary narrowness, but a conscious choice of shrinking public care and extending the vital space for private business. So the ESS today presents a picture of a bombed landscape literally: clinical, sections and units with the daily stress of functional collapse, personnel exhausted working and economic, tragic shortages of material and resources, insecurity, despair and unconsciousness of patients. Today, easy access to the doctor, even the prescribing of drugs have become a daily problem for too many people in stubbornness or rather precisely because of the monumental changes. Health poverty, exclusion and inequality in care and social care have eroded fundamental human and social rights in our country. This is unacceptable for a country in Europe and is a strong moral and political argument in the debt negotiation. That is why there is no room for continuing the same recipe and, obviously, we are not bound by the Memorandum of Understanding nor by the policy of austerity in public health expenditure, which is already at 4.5% of GDP, far below the safety limits of any serious health system. We are fully aware of the difficulties but also of the responsibility to ensure with nails and teeth and always in cooperation with doctors and other workers the survival of the ESS and the dignity of the patients. Our primary concern is to relieve society, which has suffered an unbearable burden on health, to overthrow any institutional and operational exclusion from access to reliable health care and to implement a reconstruction plan for the ESS and eradicate its timeless pathogens and distortions. It is our top political commitment and priority that insurance for all uninsured people, without exceptions, in medical surveillance, examinations, medications and hospitalization that they need. This will be done through a radical revision of the institutional framework and of the joint ministerial decisions on the care of uninsured persons. It is very important, however, that there should also be a burden relief on the daily lives of insured patients, such as the immediate abolition of the EUR 5 ticket to hospitals and health centres, the rapid cancellation of the EUR 1 communiqué per prescription and the gradual reduction of the cost of purchasing medicines. At the same time, however, an emergency plan needs to be put in place to support and strengthen the public health system with human resources. We accelerate and complete the recruitment procedures of all categories that are pending at the moment and promote a gradual coverage plan with permanent jobs of all emergency needs in primary care, hospitals and the EAW, especially in the province and in the arid island areas. With respect to the time-consuming provision of doctors and staff, we will ensure in every way that there is no unpaid overtime in the health system and that there is no observance of the state's minimum obligations. We ensure, with the necessary recruitments of doctors and nurses, the operation of all ICUs and the development of new beds, in order to eliminate the waiting list for intensive hospitalization. We are speeding up the procedures for strengthening the old fleet of the ambulances in the EMCDDA and for resolving the crucial issue of the digital upgrading of their telecommunications system. In mental health we ensure the sustainability of all units and, at the same time, the renegotiation of the terms and schedules of the Andor-Lyquoque Pact for the completion of psychiatric reform, without jeopardising continuity in the care of patients with integrated services, primary care and social care structures and always with respect for the rights of mental patients. We maintain the existing facilities for prevention and treatment of dependency, with the first aim of gradually zeroing the list for inclusion in substitution programmes. Apart from the relief of patients and ensuring the survival of the ERC, what is needed is overall reconstruction and reform in the health sector, with interventions - areas in primary care, public health, medicine, system management, the treatment of waste and corruption and the exploitation of the disease. In primary care, which should be a key pillar of the ESS, we promote a new model based on family doctors, health teams, urban health centres and neighbourhood doctors, with a view to the patient's overall health care. With scientific work, consultation and consent, we will promote a change in the institutional framework, so that there can be a real shift in primary care, prevention and health care. In public health, which is an area of falsified health policy over time, it is characteristic that only 0.15% of GDP is available, 1/3 of the average of the OECD countries, because precisely the results of public health interventions do not offer immediate micro-groups and election performances. It's an investment in the holistic model for health. It goes beyond the hospitality logic of the system and the commercialisation of medicine. It shall strengthen the prevention and health protection of society and of the country. It has a clearly developmental character, for example by upgrading environmental hygiene and the protection of the food chain and it is perhaps the most democratic - in the sense of a level playing field - of its benefits - a health policy today. In the management of the health system, we also promote the change of model by removing the party-state, the "dometerocracy" and the peltian logic in hospitals, with greater dignity and democracy, with the participation of workers, the representatives of the sick and local communities, with public accountability and social control institutions, by strengthening the democratic planning of health services against factorism and localism. We remove the “ESAN S.A.” and the business mutation of the public hospital. We review the CENs and promote full integration into the electronic system of reliable diagnostic and therapeutic protocols. Especially to combat corruption, which has always been a structural problem of the system, we need organisational, operational and institutional measures - for example, transparent management of the OSH list, autonomous CSH, imprecise and transparent procurement system, etc., - it requires reforming and tightening up the disciplinary system, without the slightest political immunity and cover-up, but above all a wider social alliance against corruption is needed in the health system, in which workers in the system, trade unions, patients' bodies and hospital administrations must take the lead. It should be a priority to preserve human dignity, equality and efficiency in care. We emphasise the protection of patients' rights and upgrade the functioning of the Citizen's Service Office to the Ministry of Health and the respective offices in hospitals, so that these services - in close cooperation and with the Citizens' Counselor - solve problems of malfunctioning the daily service of citizens in order to control the maladministration of the units and develop into a real patient's counsel. Finally, we are planning an upgraded public pharmaceutical policy, with the strengthening and reorganisation of EOF and IFET, with support for the production of quality generics in our country, with a more rational and scientifically documented utilization of public resources, ensuring that all patients have access to the medicine and a reduction in their financial burden. In particular, we consider it necessary to review, always in cooperation with the representatives of pharmacists, the entire monumental institutional framework for their operation, in order to ensure the safe and adequate disposal of medicinal products throughout the country. SYRIZA's strategic goal is to build a public and free health system of equal access and universal coverage, with high quality service and controlled costs, and this is not because of ideological obsession, but because it has been demonstrated internationally that public health systems - when of course they operate on rules and control - outweigh the quality and final cost of services and the fight against inequality in access. The private health sector obviously continues to have a distinct and complementary role, covering real needs, which cannot offer in time and sufficient the ESS, but with strict terms and conditions of cooperation, with a review of all contracts and, of course, with the State's obligations being respected. For us, the state does not exist to guarantee the proper functioning of the health market, but to ensure institutional, financial and operational the equal provision of reliable medical care and health prevention to all, regardless of the working, insurance, financial situation of everyone or the legality of staying in the country. We are reconstructing public care in the direction of a new National Health System, without the known distortions and nuisances of yesterday, a health system which retains the right of the core - which has today been largely undone - which extends the public health space and restricts the market, which enables young doctors who today emigrate abroad to offer their knowledge, which redirects public funds from the state-owned business sector to public structures, and which supports primary health care, laboratories in primary care and hospitals, the areas of proven treatment, maternity, rehabilitation and rehabilitation, which are, today, mostly privatised. [[The ending bell of the speaking time of the Deputy Minister is ringing at this point.]] In order to go ahead with such a transformation plan in health, it will be necessary, in addition to a fair taxation of real income and the necessary redistribution of wealth in the country, to actively contribute and support all honest and reliable forces of the ESS and the health world, as well as to establish another working and social ethos in the public health system. We know firsthand the difficult day-to-day life of hospitals and other health establishments. We've felt the agony of people who feed under adverse conditions for the best. We have experienced the joy of social solidarity in the field of health. We are not professional politicians or technocrats of health systems, nor are we seeking a career at the Ministry of Health. We're people of public care, we believe in her. For her dignity, her own officials and above all the patients we will strive and give all our forces consistently with words and works, with credibility, with justice, with dignity, with democracy and, of course, with the fight and support of society.

The Greek State appealed before the company itself against this decision. Obviously, the funds were filled with cuts in wages and pensions. The multinationals didn't have to pay. The state didn't stay there. He offered all possible help and facilitation to the holding company. The control mechanisms overlooked violations of national and European legislation, illegality and infringements of environmental conditions. The majority of the government took care to legislate in such a way as to serve the interests of the private company and to legalise its illegality and arbitrariness. At the same time, the disproportionate cost of environmental impacts has been called upon to bear local communities. 'They' were euphemised. In fact, they were tried to be forced and blackmailed by criminalisation and misinformation. In a place where mining has evolved into a monoculture, a part of the local society is caught and dependent on this activity. And this part has rushed to adopt and project in the last few days the media of interactivity, when for them the fired cleaners, school guards, the dynamic Halkidiki Movement were invisible. Lucky? Here too, the only argument in favour of mining is the creation of jobs, an argument that is easily destroyed. It is enough to calculate the jobs that will be lost because of mining activity. The most polluted worldwide activity competes with a position of validity with other productive and economic activities such as agriculture, livestock, tourism and others and undermines the prospects for sustainable development of the region. Finally, it does not contribute positively either to the local or national economy. The previous governments considered that investment could be carried out easily without the consent of local communities. They were wrong. No matter how hard the repression is, no matter how painful criminalization is, local communities have the final word. All this changes with our government. The environment stops being the subject of unconditional wild exploitation. Local societies stop being treated as decorative elements, without opinion and will. Investments at any cost cease to be panacea. "Sufficient crimes have been committed at the expense of our natural wealth," the Prime Minister said yesterday in the Programmatic Declarations. The current government stops the destruction of Halkidiki. At the same time, it sets a framework for economic, social and environmental reconstruction and transformation, with dialogue, planning and cooperation with the local communities, in order to stop the destruction and hostage of the place by the monoculture of the mines, with emphasis on the extremely comparative advantages of the region, the diverse natural, cultural and historical environment and the potential for sustainable development. Our position in relation to the productive model brings to the heart the needs and living conditions of citizens, environmental protection and democracy, focusing on the will of local societies. In the case of Halkidiki, the current government must move into three main axes. It is the cancellation of so-called welfare investment and a plan to protect people who are currently working in the mines and the declassification of the entire mining area. The second axis is environmental restoration, on which the above workers can work. The third axis is the reconstruction of the local economy and production, with full respect for the natural and anthropogenic environment and active participation of society. [[At this point, the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member hits a warning bell.]] Ladies and gentlemen, I will not be tired of repeating that the natural environment is common good and we must all have access to its services. It's vital space and resource. The natural environment is the condition for life and creation and we must protect it at all costs. Together with the people of Halkidiki, along with the Greek people, I am voting for the vote of confidence in this government of hope. Thank you.

Everyone recognizes education as the big patient. The measures taken are always emblematic, always with a four-year horizon - at best - because every new government thinks that it should change what the previous one has done, in order to put this signal to the great, it is assumed, a symbolic thing called "change of the educational system". Why is that? I think we can identify the reason on two points that are linked to the whole ideology of neoliberalism. Both education and culture have become tools. They're not selfish. They're tools. We are studying 'the paper'. The 'paper' is what counts, not the contents of the studies. The whole course of education at least until the university - if not after - is a course towards a paper - do you have a degree, a diploma, a doctorate? - without asking about the content, the acquisition conditions and so on. So education is the tool for the degree, because that's supposed to count on the labour market. This is not the case, ladies and gentlemen. That's not how it works. It's not that civilization will bring money. Because we cultivate culture as a result of this and as a bonus, if you like, money will come. Today the situation in education is tragic. The consequences for public health are tragic according to the annual reports of Greek and international organisations and scientific studies. Increased morbidity and mortality characterised by increasing suicide, perinatal mortality, the epidemic recurring infectious diseases that have disappeared in recent decades from the country, such as malaria, epidemics of the Western Nile virus, HIV. A dramatic reduction in vaccination coverage for children and adults with visible public health risks. This is unfortunately the situation that the new government receives in the field of health. I am sure that the measures immediately and in the long term announced by the Deputy Minister for Health will heal the wounds opened by the memorandum policies, but will also pave the way for the achievement of our strategic health objective. Key priorities include targeted staffing of health-care structures, ensuring basic, basic materials and medicines. ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u279	Thank you, Madam President. The aim for which our Government works is to organise public free-of-democratic-level education. The first stone of our programme is a worthwhile stone, that is, we are aiming to alleviate the cultural inequalities that have their roots in social class inequalities, as well as to eliminate all kinds of discrimination, gender, race, religion and beliefs, social and national origin, the physical specificity of individuals and others from the institutional framework, as well as from the everyday life of education. In this direction we intend - and the quote is not accidental - to align our institutional framework with those laid down by the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabled Persons, including disabilities in school for all. Reintroduce in an upgraded form the reinforcement of teaching and additional teaching support. To develop intercultural and anti-racist education and reception classes. To draw up programmes for support for the school population and the respective school units, mainly concerning vulnerable groups of young people, namely in difficult areas, people with financial tolerance, in Roma people, in relation to the infringability of minors. Strengthen the education of Greek children abroad, which is being seen. Let us move on to a substantial upgrading of the education of Muslim children in Thrace, in order to address school-leaving phenomena, to ensure their equal access to all levels and types of public education and to ensure the scientific training of teachers who teach this population category. We are ending the policy of dismantling and merging school units. However, we are reviewing the operation of school units which have been abolished on the basis of educational and social criteria. Ladies and gentlemen, the second stone is the satisfaction of the pressing educational needs after five years of austerity and monumental intervention. As we have said, our aim is to reconstruct public and free education of all levels, with universal participation of all young people throughout the fourteen-year range - that is, twelve plus two pre-school education. In particular: Firstly, the memorial laws that have dissolved and degraded public education cease to apply. We are moving towards the establishment of a transitional institutional framework for all levels of education, opening, as the Prime Minister and the Minister of Education said, a broad dialogue with all involved. In cooperation with other co-legislative ministries, we are moving towards measures to relieve the humanitarian crisis, as the Deputy Minister, Mr Fotio, said, through preventive medicine, vaccination and food aid programmes, namely breakfast and small meals for students. We are starting today to redesign the detailed programmes and the school manuals. We aim to restructure the matter in the direction of reducing the volume of information for the sake of deepening and critical thought. Our aim is for the books next school year to be in time in schools and any changes in matter will not necessarily mean changing the school manuals next year, because we do not have time. We are exploring all the necessary conditions for achieving a reduction in the number of pupils per section, of course, insofar as the opportunities are to cover the places of training with permanent posts. I repeat: with permanent positions. At the school we have all nursed, we know that some lessons are primates and some secondary ones. For us primates are all those lessons that shape the prerequisites for becoming tomorrow's citizens educated, with critical thought and aesthetic culture. To this end we want our worthy place to cover general education in the public school of all knowledge and experience to get flesh and bone. To this end, aesthetic education, the cultivation of arts, physical education, health education, environmental education, information technology, the education of the democratic citizen shall be designed as equal and necessary aspects of the analytical programme together with traditional circular knowledge. We are working towards upgrading and gradually reintroducing the teaching of more foreign languages at school, according to pedagogical criteria. We want the possibility of obtaining certificates of language instruction from the public school so that the family budget can be relieved. Finally, in this section, we make the school center of the neighborhood, the social area of creation, open and after the end of the courses for cultural, sport, social solidarity and others, in cooperation with the educational community, the residents of the neighborhood and local authorities. In particular, for high school we strengthen the autonomous cultural role of the lyric, with the restoration of the essential equivalence - in general and technical professional direction - and our strategic aim is the single school of theory and practice, making use of the positive features of the experience of multi-classical high school. Thus, as the Minister of Education mentioned, we are launching the disconnection of pre-school and secondary examinations from the process of introduction to higher education. We are reintroducing the general average of 9.5 for promotion from one class to another. We remove the pan-Hellenic exams in the 1st and 2nd grades from the current school year, as we abolish the mandatory selection of 50% of the themes of the high school exams from the themes bank. As we have said, the bank will remain an advisory tool for teachers and students. Let us note that the money spent on the topic bank was EUR 1,570,000. With this money, a thousand alternates could have been hired to deal with part of the educational gaps. As mentioned by the Minister of Education, in cooperation with the relevant ministries, the reintroduction of school guards that have been made available is under way. We plan to upgrade public technical vocational education and reorganise the ESDP programmes. We provide equal access to higher education for their graduates. We are abolishing post-training TECs, i.e. schools of vocational training and remodeling the post-mortem year of PSAs from the year of apprenticeship, in the year of qualification of PSA graduates and re-establishing them from informal in formal education. They are reopening, in accordance with our commitments, the PSAs from the school year 2015-2016, the sectors and specialities of PSAs and PSAs that were abolished by Law.4146/2013. The teachers of the fifty specialties of technical vocational education repealed by Article 82 of Law No 4172/2013 shall be placed directly in the PES. For this year, you need to know that there are teachers who were paid with 75% and stayed in their homes. These teachers return and exploit for the educational and administrative needs of public schools in laboratory centres, in areas that can work, in IEKs and in the Ministry of Education services. Particularly for this year's pan-Hellenic examinations, as mentioned by the Minister of Education, and for the next two school years, we will ensure that the educational functions are not disturbed and that there are some urgent issues which facilitate them. So, first of all, for this school year, the examination procedures and arrangements concerning the dismissal from high school and the introduction to higher education of the students and students in the third grade remain the same. Secondly, for the third high school year of next year, in other words for students in the second grade, we are studying the possibility of communication - and it will soon be announced - of the scientific fields of specialisation so that candidates can choose more than one scientific field. The promotion grades of the 1st and 2nd grades and the degree of dismissal of the 3rd grades will not be counted to the level of access to higher education. Finally, we are opening up the dialogue with society on the new system of introduction to the HEIs, which will obviously take several years in the prospect of free access to higher education. Preprimary and primary education: We lay firm foundations for a public quality pre-school and primary education, which will provide all the necessary facilities for students and lay the foundations for the drastic reduction of school failure in the next grades. Ladies and gentlemen, we can assure the Greek people that we will do everything necessary to ensure that all children have uninterrupted access to public free two-year pre-school education throughout Greece. We emphasise the necessary infrastructure and staffing with the necessary staff. We ensure the quality upgrade of primary and full-day school. The desert villages without schools and the school units "mumuth" in urban areas are images that do not satisfy us and do not comply with modern teaching. Finally, in this section, the recent circular of the previous Minister of Education, on the change of the timetable since March 2015, is being revoked. Special treatment. We should all know and have embraced the fact that the training of disabled or even special educational needs is a fundamental indicator of culture. The declared ‘one school for all’ principle refers to the neighborhood school, in which all children with or without disabilities are enrolled and supported, staffed, equipped and formulated, so that the objective of integration and inclusion is consistently achieved. Of course there will also be special schools dealing with children whose kind of disability or their multiple disabilities do not allow them to join the public school. But we should stress that these structures are designed for personalized cognitive, recreational and sports programmes tailored to the needs of each student and support services are provided to both their students and their families. We will see to it that the institutionalised diagnostic bodies of the CEDEFOP and CEDEFOP are fully upgraded and supported, and that is our priority. We will proceed from this year to the implementation of the single recruitment table of substitutes for special educational and special assistant staff, as well as the rehabilitation of teachers serving the structures of special education and being made available by the abolition of their branches. Parallel support is granted to the disabled learner regardless of the existence of a school membership section. Too many parents are worried. We have announced this since the previous parliamentary term. The Special Education Act will be drafted from zero to zero with consideration of the proposals of all stakeholders. In the case of the education of children with disabilities and special educational needs, we want to have parents allies and partners. As far as private education is concerned, I would like to highlight three points. Firstly, we are supervising, as Article 16 of our Charter of Fundamental Rights requires, the maintenance of constitutional legitimacy in the establishment and functioning of private schools. Secondly, we are reforming and updating the legal framework governing the operation of private schools and schools on the basis of educational and scientific criteria and we are committed to its absolute compliance. Thirdly, we are ensuring, for reasons of social and public interest, the working rights of private teachers who return to the Ministry of Education and who cease to fall within the Ministry of Labour. We are again here, as in other categories of workers in the private sector, the institution of collective agreements. Finally, in this section, we are reconstructing the abstract private education management of the Ministry of Education. Ladies and gentlemen, the creation of a climate of cooperation and mutual trust between the state and the educational community together with the continuous free - I stress - training of teachers and democratic reconstruction of education are a safe prerequisite for the success of our efforts. The current procedures, as the Minister said, have not contributed to the creation of such a climate and have injured the educational community. This is why they are suspended, as are any disciplinary or promotional consequences, in order to examine in depth all relevant factors. The replacement of the current legislative framework for the evaluation of teachers and schools by a system of evaluation of educational and teaching work in a democratic and collective manner is necessary. However, we realise - and take care of this - that the operational assessment of educational work is a participatory and democratic methodology for the diagnosis of problems, for solutions and for the preparation of proposals and measures to improve the quality of education. Under no circumstances is it a tool for the hierarchical and financial distinction between school units or a penalty for wage differentiation and administrative discipline of 'officials', as we considered them to be. He'll tell you tomorrow and Mr. Katrugalos that we're abolishing all the disciplinary legislative mesh of n. Date of birth: 4057/2012 and No 4093/2012. Up to its overall reform, the anti-constitutional arrangements of the Disciplinary Law shall be immediately abolished, such as the provision that the staff member wishes on a holiday or a referral to the Disciplinary Board and the provision laying down as an offence manifestly inappropriate or inappropriate or unworthy conduct in or out of service. We recognise that participation in trade union or other social activity is in no way an inappropriate behaviour. We ensure the transparency of procedures in all service changes as mentioned by the Minister. Our Government will not tolerate any of the well-known practices of a clientele of a pedagogical and administrative authoritarian expression of the days and the reward of the minor effort. I want us to work together openly and honestly with everyone.
