And unfortunately, tens of thousands of people have not been able to see at the moment of joy and share with us the optimism that we are breathing into the new reality that is being shaped. And I want to say that in my area, in the Cyclades there is really no historical precedent for the Left three of the four Members. And of course, there was not even a precedent in 2012 in the modern history of Greece, for the Left MEP to come to me to have this honour. Now, of course, together with the other two colleagues from the Cyclades, we are taking a huge responsibility for the work that we will support and bring to completion together the whole parliamentary group and all the people. I would like to say that our islands Greece and the Cyclades, over the last few months, in addition to the wage cuts, pensions, tax raid and the other horizontal measures that have been in existence by the Memorandum governments, have suffered a great deal of damage, which is, in fact, equivalent to the abolition of forty-year conquests for decentralisation and regional development. They closed down services, public financial services, closed up structures, IKAs, Labour Inspectorates and a number of other services. The public health system collapses, education works in a problematic way, self-government has become a public service in the performance of day-to-day tasks. The ferry interconnection operates at two speeds, a winter, which is extremely problematic and one that goes relatively well. The maritime transport has suffered severe reductions in the routes due to a cut in the funds for the so-called ‘problems of public interest’. Tourism, although it is upward - upward, of course, not by Greek tourists but by foreign tourists - cannot compensate for the cut-out and isolation of many islands from the centres of the island prefectures and the mainland, it cannot compensate for the hardship of moving the permanent residents who for reasons of facilitation and solution to tax or insurance problems, are obliged to move outside their island, cannot compensate for the lack of doctors of specialties, cannot compensate for unemployment in large islands such as Syros, where the Neorio Shipyard is growing, professionals shut down or give a livelihood, public officials cannot live with hunger wages. In the face of this situation, the Government of Social Rescue must include the solution to the problems of island status, which is a prerequisite for the European Treaties and the Greek Constitution. Our Government must work together with the islands and local communities, measures to implement the island specificity, in addition to the reduced VAT rates in the Aegean Sea and the maintenance of low rates in tourism. These are unnecessarily, if we want island Greece to have a sustainable development perspective in all areas with sovereign, of course, sustainable quality tourism. Among the crucial issues relating to the sustainable development of island Greece, two are the most crucial, without undermining the rest. The first is the coastline issue. Its effects have already been apparent immediately after the new leadership took responsibility for the Ministry of Shipping and the Aegean, such as the end of the one-circuit contract, the damage of the "MYTILINE" ship that operates routes to Samos and the northern Aegean Sea, the adjunct to "PANAGIA TOOU", formerly "AGIOS GEORGIOS" which now makes the line Kythira - Antikythera due to non-paids. These phenomena are the results of a more unsuccessful and bankrupt coastal model based exclusively on private companies and having the most expensive tickets from all European Union countries. Unfortunately, the treatment of maritime interconnectors cannot be radically solved in time beyond micro-improvements, except by designing and setting up the public boarding body that will require at least a few months to implement. It will be an entity which, firstly, will design the minimum public transport network and, secondly, will create the framework for the provision of the transport project either by vessels of the operator itself or by leasing ships from private ferry companies. The second crucial issue is public health. Specialisation doctors or agricultural doctors may not continue to be under-store or staffed exclusively, which change every fortnight. It cannot be done with a former fisherman and cost EUR 3 to 4000. Also, there may be no ambulances or even drivers of the EMCDDA. Our proposal, which the head of the Ministry of Health must directly deal with, is the support of three hospitals in the Cyclades, which must be done first by upgrading the Syros and Naxos Hospitals, but also by joining the ESS and opening the Santorini Hospital, the staffing of health centers and regional doctors, providing incentives, the creation of a regional NSAB, the operation of a telemedicine system in the small islands. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I would like a slight tolerance, Madam President. From there on, there are some other issues that I am emphatizing. Support for ship repair and among them the New Town Shipyard, the stop of industrial wind farms on four islands, Tinos, Andros, Paros and Naxos, which will destroy the landscape and tourism and will have no benefit for local communities, the construction of special land-based land-based facilities at the new airport of Paros, the rating and support of the Marble-sculptures School in Tinos, the reintroduction of the former status of popular administration in Pan-Hellenic Holy Institute of Tinos, support for rural and livestock production, with a view to reducing the cost of production and handling of products, support for the coastal fishing industry, with the exception of fishermen from the mandatory installation of electronic and satellite systems, as provided for by the European Regulation and the creation of fisheries protection areas, the extension of trademarks and tax offices. To conclude, Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to point out this: we are fully aware of the difficulties and the responsibilities that we have. Our struggle will be titanium, but in the end we will succeed. And we will succeed, because the soul of our people is also speaking with us. The first answer will come on Wednesday to the squares and streets of Greece. Thank you.
ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u300	Thank you, Mr. Armalene. Mr. Mavrotas from the river.
ParlaMint-GR_2015-02-09-S1-commons.u301	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, the brief speech I am going to make will focus on the government positions on education, as formulated by the Prime Minister's programme statements, but also by the Minister and the Deputy Minister - it is a happy juncture that is here this evening so late to listen to us. I will not go along with general considerations, but I shall refer to specific issues. Our main positions as Potami have, after all, been published for a long time and are on our site. First of all, there are a number of points on which we are converging with the positions of government, but there are many points to which we differ. The points we agree on are, in particular, pre-school, primary and secondary education, and are mainly focused on the key issues of higher education. We agree with the need for universal pre-school education, the upgrading of teachers, the view of high school as an autonomous level of education, and not as preparation for the introduction to universities, the possibility of certification for foreign language knowledge and the use of computers from the middle education, which was mentioned by the Deputy Minister, the strengthening of technical education, as well as others. We would like to insist on average education and primary schooling in some other areas, such as evaluation not with punishment but as an essential element of measuring the performance and evolution of the system, establishing and upgrading independent principles such as, for example, the Institute for Educational Policy, with a remarkable and not a party origin, the emphasis on school administration, decapitated by party mechanisms. Finally, we would like to insist on extending the institution of school standards, which is a part of which public education can be proud of. We disagree with the abolition of introductory tests in standards, because we believe that we should not be 'allergers' in concepts such as excellence. We may all be equal, but we're not the same. Besides, there are standards everywhere in the world, and they're with tests. As far as higher education is concerned, I shall begin with the only mention made by the Prime Minister of the abolition of the provisions on institution councils. The Prime Minister said that they are destroying the selfishness of universities. Evidently, however, some have misled him, since the members of the Foundation councils are actually elected, as is the case with the budgets, and they were elected three years ago with a great deal of participation. Participation in these elections was 79% in the Kapodistrian, 66% in the Metsovio Polytechnics and 77% in Aristotle. Institutional councils are generally decorated by international prestige scientists, among other things, that is, the foaming of what the Prime Minister has said - quite rightly - wants to repatriate. So the abolition of the institution councils and this message "speaking" that passes, gives them a signal that "principals, it is not worth the effort to deal with the Greek university" and that is not good in our opinion. Also, the institution councils were a window of society towards universities, as they participated in them in addition to universities, social bodies and institutions of local society and their abolition suggests that we want a university locked in its glass, without interacting with society. They are also a test of scrutiny of the decisions of the administration concerned, which will promote accountability and transparency, which are also essential. Generally, the Minister said that Law No.4009/2011, the Diamantopoulou law as we are used to calling it, was and is destructive. We consider it necessary to amend its identified failures, but we are not in favour of its abolition. The reintroduction to the 1982 framework law, as some SYRIZA officials are proclaiming, is a setback for us. Others were the needs in 1982, thirty - three years ago, other needs today. And the association with the European and global awareness of universities is necessary for us as a one-way path. We believe in the participation of students in decision-making, but this is done in an institutionalised way and not arbitrarily with self-determination groups which invade the congress or assembly of schools. Dynamic minorities operating in association must be replaced by the institutional representatives representing the student movement. Also, contrary to SYRIZA's consistent position, we are in favour of the revision of Article 16, so that non-governmental, non-profit universities can be set up, but with specific standards that will be under the control of the Quality Assurance Authority in education.(ii) like the public universities, which we clearly consider to be better than the current blurred setting with the various CECs and so on. The effects will be positive, not only in the economic but also in the intellectual balance of the country, because Greek scientists and students will remain in our country. And, incidentally, we think that the country's intellectual balance, that is, most exports from imports, is a greater risk than the deficit trade balance, which the Prime Minister himself recognised in his speech. On the same lines as limiting brain leakage and combating the major problem of unemployment, it is also the further interconnection of universities with production, as well as the strengthening of innovation with entrepreneurship. I mean, don't stay on the shelf. Given that most companies in Greece are small and without research and development, we believe that the further interconnection of universities with production will be mutually beneficial. We believe that the Greek university should break its ties with the past that holds it hostage and meet the current challenges to prepare for the future. The materials are there. We have seen how Greek teachers and students differ abroad, but we must also follow the correct recipe. To follow practices that will bring back Greek scientists and students and not practices that will expel others, not only because of economic conditions but also because of customer logic. As Mr Baltas said, we need to focus on the substance and get away from utilities. In fact, as my mentor at the polytechnic, the late Lefteris Papayannakis, is saying, we will finally get to the letters. In other words, degrees will reflect the actual content of the studies and will not be papers that are especially easily faded today. In conclusion, I would like to wish the new government every success, which must be understood in time of the criticalness of the country's situation. Let me stress that the river will not make a opposition not at all. It will be present to monitor and monitor effectively and constructively governmental work with responsibility, with concrete proposals and arguments and documentation, without unnecessary and vague politics. Thank you.
